1. RESTful API v2 Specification

1.1. Authentication

The HTTP/HTTPS authentication should be used, for example with curl it is possible to specify username and password with -u <user>:<password>

Using HTTPS is recommended for security. See this post to enable HTTPS.

1.2. Request Format

Parameters can be provided both using GET with a query string or POST using JSON (in this case please make sure the correct Content Type is provided). For example, to download data for a host you can use the below curl command line using GET:

curl -s -u admin:admin "http://192.168.1.1:3000/lua/rest/v2/get/host/data.lua?ifid=1&host=192.168.1.2"

or the below curl command line using POST:

curl -s -u admin:admin -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"ifid": "1", "host": "192.168.1.2"}' "http://192.168.1.1:3000/lua/rest/v2/get/host/data.lua"

Please check the Examples section for more examples.

1.3. Response Format

An API response is usually represented by a JSON message matching a standard structure. This JSON message consists of an envelope containing:

  • a return code rc

  • a human-readable string rc_str describing the return code

  • the actual response in rsp

Example:

{
 "rc": 0
 "rc_str": "OK",
 "rsp": {
    ...
 }
}

1.4. API

1.4.1. Interfaces

POST /lua/pro/rest/v2/add/interface/db_flows.lua

Analyse historical flows as live flows

  • Description: Loads the historical flows matching the time range and the flow filters (the same parameters of the Historical Flows page) into a runtime interface named Database, limited by the flow cache size. Answers { new_ifid, max_num_flows }. Administrators only, ClickHouse.

  • Consumes: [‘application/json’]

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

body

body

Responses

0 - OK

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE or NOT_ENABLED (no ClickHouse)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED (administrators only)

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

-6 - INTERNAL_ERROR (the interface could not be created)

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/lightview/aggregate.lua

Aggregate a REST endpoint across all interfaces (Overview)

  • Description: Used by the Overview (lightview) mode: forwards the request to the specified REST endpoint once per local interface (setting ifid accordingly) and merges the results according to the dashboard component type. All the other query parameters are forwarded to the endpoint, except ifid. Enterprise only.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

endpoint

query

Path of the REST endpoint to query on each interface (e.g. /lua/rest/v2/get/interface/l7/stats.lua)

string

component

query

How to merge the per-interface results: pie, badge, table, sankey or timeseries. When omitted, the result of the endpoint is returned as is (the last interface queried).

string

max_values

query

Maximum number of slices for the pie component (default 10)

integer

Responses

0 - OK. Merged data, in the same format as the forwarded endpoint (empty if endpoint is not set)

POST /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/lightview/aggregate.lua

Aggregate a REST endpoint across all interfaces (Overview), with a request body

  • Description: Same as GET, for endpoints that take a JSON body. With endpoint=/lua/rest/v2/get/timeseries/batch.lua, each query in the body is run on every interface (the ifid in ts_query is replaced) and the series are summed into one serie per query; queries with “split”: true also return one serie per interface (split_series).

  • Consumes: [‘application/json’]

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

endpoint

query

Path of the REST endpoint to query on each interface

string

component

query

How to merge the per-interface results (see GET)

string

payload

body

JSON body forwarded to the endpoint (e.g. the queries of timeseries/batch.lua)

Responses

0 - OK. Merged data, in the same format as the forwarded endpoint

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/traffic_report/data.lua

Get the traffic report of an interface, network or host

  • Description: Traffic of each hour (report=daily) or day (weekly, monthly) of the period containing epoch_begin, next to the same bucket of the previous period: rows [{ tstamp, bytes, activity (active seconds), prev_bytes, prev_activity }], with epoch_begin/epoch_end of the period and the start of the previous/next period. Reads the RRD timeseries: Enterprise M, RRD timeseries driver, not on pcap files.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

host

query

Host (IP, with @vlan for the VLAN hosts); default the interface

string

network

query

Local network identifier; default the interface

integer

report

query

Period

string

epoch_begin

query

An epoch inside the period (default now)

integer

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND (no traffic timeseries for the entity)

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE (missing ifid, or an interface the user cannot see) or NOT_ENABLED (no RRD driver, or pcap file)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED (license, or a host/network outside the allowed networks of the user)

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS (unknown report or network)

1.4.2. Hosts

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/charts/host/map.lua

Get the hosts map data of an interface

  • Description: Enterprise only. Up to 64 local and 64 remote active hosts placed by the chosen criteria. rsp.series: [{name: local|remote, base_url (host details page), data: [{x, y, z (bubble radius in px, 3-33, from the host traffic), meta: {label (host name or IP), url_query (host=<ip>@<vlan>)}}]}]. x/y per bubble_mode: 0 flows as server/client, 1 unreachable flows as server/client, 2 alerted flows as server/client, 3 DNS positive replies/sent queries, 4 SYN sent/received, 5 SYN sent/RST received, 6 SYN sent/SYN-ACK received, 7 TCP packets sent/received, 8 TCP bytes sent/received, 9 active alerted flows as server/client, 10 bytes/packets ratio, 11 client/server score, 12 blacklisted flows as client/server, 13 unidirectional TCP flows as client/server. Modes 4-6 need a packet interface.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

bubble_mode

query

Criteria, 0-13 (default 0)

integer

Responses

0 - OK

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/interface/local_hosts_report.lua

Get the local hosts activity report

  • Description: Traffic of the local hosts (assets) in a time range, from the host timeseries (live data when epoch_begin = epoch_end). Each row: ip, vlan, host (ip@vlan), host_key, name, online, bytes_sent, bytes_rcvd, local_traffic, remote_traffic, total_traffic, availability (traffic per time slot) and max_value_per_point (max of each slot over the page). Enterprise M only.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

epoch_begin

query

Start of the time range (epoch seconds). Equal to epoch_end = live data

integer

epoch_end

query

End of the time range (epoch seconds)

integer

preset

query

Time range preset, sets the number of availability points: hour (12), day (24), week (7), month (30), year (12) (optional)

string

host_visibility

query

Hosts listed: only_traffic (default), no_traffic, visible, hidden (optional)

string

start

query

Pagination: index of the first host

integer

length

query

Pagination: number of hosts

integer

sort

query

Sort column: ip, name, bytes_sent, bytes_rcvd, local_traffic, remote_traffic, total_traffic (optional)

string

order

query

Sort order: asc or desc (optional)

string

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND (not Enterprise M)

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE (missing time range, or an interface the user cannot access)

Hide or show a host in the local hosts activity report

  • Description: Hides a host from the local hosts activity report (listed again with host_visibility=hidden) or shows it again. Enterprise M and administrators only.

  • Consumes: [‘application/json’]

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/host/asset_details.lua

Get the details of an asset

  • Description: Details of an asset of the inventory as raw values: ip, vlan, mac, name, title, device_type (id), manufacturer, model, os_id/os_name, first_seen/last_seen (epochs), online, dhcp, snmp_name, network { id, name }, tags [{ id, name, color }], names [{ source, name }], switch { ip, name, port, port_name, trunk, num_ports }, server_ports { tcp, udp: [{ port, name }] }, wazuh (agent: status, version, os, cpu, ram, open_ports, ifaces). Enterprise M; assets of hosts outside the allowed networks are not shown.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

serial_key

query

Asset key (key field of rest/v2/get/host/assets.lua)

string

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS (no serial_key)

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

1.4.3. Alerts

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/acknowledge/snmp/device/alerts.lua

Acknowledge SNMP device alerts

  • Description: DEPRECATED: alerts are acknowledged through the issues (edit/issue/acknowledge.lua, Enterprise XXL+). Acknowledge SNMP device historical alerts

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

label

query

Describe why the alert was ackowledge, it can be an empty string

string

alert_id

query

Alert identifier (format: ‘id;eq’, where ‘id’ is the id and ‘eq’ stands for ‘equal’, currently available: ‘eq’, ‘neq’)

integer

row_id

query

Alert identifier of the exact alert

integer

epoch_begin

query

Start time (epoch)

integer

epoch_end

query

End time (epoch)

integer

severity

query

Severity identifier (format: ‘id;eq’, where ‘id’ is the amount of traffic and ‘eq’ stands for ‘equal’, currently available: ‘eq’ -> equal, ‘neq’ -> not equal, ‘lt’ -> lesser then, ‘lte’ -> lesser or equal then, ‘gt’ -> greater then, ‘gte’ -> greater or equal then

integer

score

query

Score (format: ‘id;eq’, where ‘id’ is the amount of traffic and ‘eq’ stands for ‘equal’, currently available: ‘eq’ -> equal, ‘neq’ -> not equal, ‘lt’ -> lesser then, ‘lte’ -> lesser or equal then, ‘gt’ -> greater then, ‘gte’ -> greater or equal then

integer

ip

query

IP (format: ‘id;eq’, where ‘id’ is the id and ‘eq’ stands for ‘equal’, currently available: ‘eq’, ‘neq’)

string

snmp_interface

query

SNMP interface identifier (format: ‘id;eq’, where ‘id’ is the id and ‘eq’ stands for ‘equal’, currently available: ‘eq’, ‘neq’)

string

Responses

0 - OK

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

  • Description: Flow alerts of the last timeframe grouped by client/server/alert type (at most 100 groups): one series per alert type (or one series for the score modes), each point x/y/z (z = bubble radius) with meta.label and meta.url_query (filters for /lua/alert_stats.lua). Position

Description

Type

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/flow/historical/overview.lua

Get the details of a historical flow

  • Description: One flow stored in ClickHouse: rsp.flow is the record (raw columns, e.g. SRC2DST_BYTES, IPV4_SRC_ADDR, CLIENT_NW_LATENCY_US), rsp.proto_info the decoded PROTOCOL_INFO_JSON (protocol fields, TCP flags analysis, QoE, exporters, BGP), rsp.info the formatted fields (host names, ports, AS, countries, pools, connection states) and rsp.ext what the server resolves: tags, MAC manufacturers, AS/site/exporter/observation point names, the issues (alert id, nDPI risk id, source, score, title, info, MITRE id and tactic), the additional exported fields and the protocol views of the flow (views: modbus, s7comm, profinet, bgp, hr_charts; view_reasons: view -> license or no_data for the views shown disabled). Needs ClickHouse and the historical flows permission; flows of hosts outside the allowed networks of the user are not found.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

row_id

query

Row id of the flow (rowid of the historical flows list)

integer

tstamp

query

Timestamp of the flow (tstamp of the historical flows list)

integer

instance_name

query

ntopng instance that stored the flow (NTOPNG_INSTANCE_NAME)

string

bgp

query

true: also rsp.ext.bgp_info (BGP/BMP information of the peers)

boolean

llm

query

true: also rsp.ext.llm_flow_data (the flow for the nAnalyst assistant)

boolean

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/dashboard/widget_data.lua

Get the data of a widget

  • Description: Runs the SQL query of a saved widget on ClickHouse and returns its result, shaped for the widget type (e.g. stat: { value }). The placeholders $EPOCH_BEGIN$, $EPOCH_END$, $IFID$ and $FILTERS$ of the query are replaced with the parameters below; on the flows tables the flow filters (same parameters as the Historical Flows explorer, e.g. l7proto=7;eq) are applied too. Administrators only.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Name

Position

Description

Type

integer

integer

ifid

integer

POST /lua/pro/rest/v2/set/dashboard/dashboard.lua

Create or update an AI dashboard

  • Description: Creates a dashboard (id derived from the name) or, when dashboard_id matches an existing one, replaces it. Returns { success, id, dashboard }. The field names differ from the stored ones: dashboard_name (not name), widget_id (not id) and layout { pos_x, pos_y, grid_w, grid_h }. Widget types: timeseries, table, bar_chart, pie_chart, stat, heatmap, bubble_chart, sankey, chord. Administrators only.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

payload

body

JSON body, e.g. { “dashboard_name”: “Network overview”, “description”: “”, “widgets”: [{ “widget_id”: “w1”, “type”: “stat”, “title”: “Flows”, “config”: { “sql_query”: “SELECT count() AS value FROM flows WHERE FIRST_SEEN >= $EPOCH_BEGIN$ AND FIRST_SEEN <= $EPOCH_END$ AND INTERFACE_ID = $IFID$ AND $FILTERS$”, “value_field”: “value”, “unit”: “number” }, “layout”: { “pos_x”: 0, “pos_y”: 0, “grid_w”: 4, “grid_h”: 3 } }] }. Add “dashboard_id” to update an existing dashboard.

Responses

0 - OK

-3 - NOT_GRANTED (administrators only)

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

-59 - MISSING_PARAMETERS

-8 - BAD_CONTENT (invalid definition, the reason is in rsp.error)

Position

Description

Type

formData

formData

integer

formData

boolean

formData

string

formData

string

syslog_rule_comment

formData

Free-text description. Max 255 characters, no control characters

string

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/pool/policy.lua

Get the traffic policy of a host pool

  • Description: Default policy and rules (nDPI markers) of a host pool, with the values usable in the rules (options: [{ value, label }] per kind) and the markers. Rules by kind: l7_proto (nDPI protocol id), l7_category (nDPI category id), host (host name or IP), country (ISO code), continent (code), risk (flow risk id), each [{ value, marker }]; marker = pass | 3 (slow) | 2 (slower) | drop (risks always drop). Pro (not nEdge), pools capability.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/asn/get_as_data.lua

Get the autonomous systems of an interface with their traffic

  • Description: Live: the active ASes of the interface (since startup). Historical (is_live=false, needs ClickHouse): the ASes of the flows in [epoch_begin, epoch_end]. rsp is an array of records: asn, asname (none for ASN 0, the private hosts), num_hosts, avg_host_score, score, alerted_flows, seen_since (epoch), bytes_sent, bytes_rcvd, traffic, throughput (bps), breakdown {bytes_sent, bytes_rcvd}, breakdown_role {bytes_other, bytes_transit, bytes_peering, bytes_ix}, is_customer_asn / is_sub_customer_asn / is_remote_asn (ASN configuration), is_in_memory (historical only: the AS has active hosts now).

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

integer

is_live

query

true (default) live data, false historical data (ClickHouse)

boolean

epoch_begin

query

Historical only: start of the time range (epoch)

integer

epoch_end

query

Historical only: end of the time range (epoch)

integer

show_as

query

all (default), my_as, my_customer_as, remote_as or other_as (ASN configuration)

string

interface_role

query

Enterprise M: all (default), ix, peering or transit (SNMP interface role)

string

Responses

0 - OK

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/flow/live/overview.lua

Get the details of a live flow

  • Description: One active flow: rsp.flow is the flow as exported by the engine (raw keys, e.g. cli2srv.bytes, protos.tls.client_requested_server_name, tcp.nw_latency.3wh_client_rtt), rsp.ext what the server resolves: host names (cli_name, srv_name), VLAN alias, tags, AS/site/exporter/observation point names, the issues (alert id, nDPI risk id, source, score, i18n key of the title, info, MITRE id and tactic), SIP/RTP values, the additional exported fields with their description, the protocol views of the flow (views: modbus, bgp, s7comm, profinet) and the user capabilities (is_admin, can_disable_check). Flows of hosts outside the allowed networks of the user are not found.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Position

integer

counters

query

boolean

bgp

query

true: also rsp.ext.bgp_info (BGP/BMP information of the peers)

boolean

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/geo_map/hosts.lua

Get the geolocated hosts of an interface

  • Description: The active hosts with a known location (latitude/longitude from the geolocation database), at most 512. rsp is an array of records: ip, name, vlan {id, name}, lat, lng, country (ISO 3166 alpha-3), country_code (alpha-2), country_id, city (when known), scoreClient, scoreServer, numAlerts (alerted active flows), isAlert (score > 0), isRoot. Any user can call it (interface and network restrictions apply).

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

hosts_category

query

0 active hosts by traffic (default), 1 alerted hosts by score, 2 local hosts, 3 remote hosts

integer

host

query

When set, the hosts the given host is talking with (active flows) instead of the category

string

Responses

0 - OK

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/host/active_list.lua

Get the active hosts list

  • Description: Active hosts of the interface, one page at a time, with the filters of the Hosts page. Each row: host (ip, vlan, mac, os, country and flags), hostname, first_seen, last_seen, throughput, bytes, alerts, num_flows, score, isBlocked (nEdge), location (nEdge). recordsTotal is the number of hosts matching the filters. Only the hosts of the networks the user is allowed to see are listed.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier (optional, default: the selected one). An interface the user cannot access answers INVALID_INTERFACE

integer

start

query

Pagination: index of the first host (optional)

integer

length

query

Pagination: number of hosts (optional)

integer

sort

query

Sort column: ip_address, hostname, vlan, num_flows, alerts, score, first_seen, throughput, bytes (optional)

string

order

query

Sort order: asc or desc (optional)

string

map_search

query

Text search on the hosts (optional)

string

mode

query

Hosts filter: local, local_no_tx, local_no_tcp_tx, remote, remote_no_tx, remote_no_tcp_tx, broadcast_domain, broadcast_multicast, filtered, blacklisted, dhcp (optional)

string

version

query

IP version: 4 or 6 (optional)

integer

traffic_type

query

Direction: one_way or bidirectional (optional)

string

vlan

query

VLAN id (optional)

integer

network

query

Local network id (optional)

integer

network_cidr

query

Network in CIDR format (optional)

string

pool

query

Host pool id (optional)

integer

asn

query

Autonomous System number (optional)

integer

country

query

Country code, e.g. IT (optional)

string

os

query

Operating system id (optional)

integer

mac

query

MAC address (optional)

string

protocol

query

nDPI application protocol id (optional)

integer

deviceIP

query

Flow exporter IP (optional)

string

location

query

nEdge only: lan, wan or unknown (optional)

string

label

query

integer

  • Description: The ICMP messages of an active host per version (ICMPv4, ICMPv6): “<type>,<code>” -> packets sent and rcvd, last_host_sent_peer and last_host_rcvd_peer.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

host

query

Host address (IP or IP@VLAN if traffic is tagged)

string

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/host/dns.lua

Get the DNS counters of a host

  • Description: The DNS counters of an active host (dns.sent / dns.rcvd: num_queries, num_replies_ok, num_replies_error and the queries per type) and the DoH/DoT servers it used (doh_dot: ip, vlan_id, num_uses).

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

host

query

string

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/host/http.lua

Get the HTTP counters of a host

  • Description: The HTTP counters of an active host: requests sent per method (http.sender.query), responses received per code (http.receiver.response) and the virtual hosts it served (http.virtual_hosts: bytes sent/rcvd, requests).

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/host/overview.lua

Get the overview of a host

  • Description: An active host as raw values (host): identity and flags (MAC, router, VLAN, OS, ASN, network, country, local/private/multicast/broadcast/blacklisted…), first/last seen, bytes and packets sent/rcvd, local/non-local bytes, score as client/server with its network/security breakdown, active/total/alerted/unreachable flows, blacklisted and unidirectional TCP flows, peers, unresponsive peers, contacted servers, TCP retransmissions/out of order/lost/keep-alive, alerts, names, OS learning, flow exporter. ext: pool, symbolic MAC and device type, local network alias and site, host tags (name, or name_i18n for the built-in ones), asset key, ICMP active monitoring state, notes, page flags. counters=true: only host.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

host

query

Host address (IP or IP@VLAN if traffic is tagged)

string

counters

query

true: only the host values (no ext), for the live updates

boolean

Responses

0 - OK

-1 - NOT_FOUND

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/host/assets_journal.lua

Get the assets journal entries

  • Description: Entries of the assets journal in a time interval, newest first: event_time and created_time (epochs), asset (key, type, ip, vlan, name), who, action, subaction, message. Enterprise L, assets inventory and journal preference enabled

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier (default: the current interface)

integer

serial_key

query

Key of an asset: only its entries (default: all the assets of the interface)

string

epoch_begin

query

Begin of the interval, on the time of the events (epoch, default: epoch_end - 1 day)

integer

epoch_end

query

End of the interval, on the time of the events (epoch, default: now)

integer

action

query

Only the entries with this action (Baseline, New, Update, Delete, Manual)

string

who

query

Only the entries caused by this user (ntopng for the automatic events)

string

subaction

query

Only the entries about this subaction (e.g. os, tag, mac)

string

start

query

Offset of the first entry of the page (default: 0)

integer

length

query

Number of entries of the page (default: all the entries)

integer

sort

query

Column to sort by: event_time (default), created_time, asset, action, subaction, who

string

order

query

Sort order: asc or desc (default)

string

map_search

query

Free text (case insensitive): key (IP of the asset), who, action, subaction and message of the entries

string

Responses

0 - OK: the entries of the page in rsp, the number of the entries matching (all the pages) in recordsTotal and recordsFiltered

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/host/pool/list.lua

Get the host pools of an interface with their traffic

  • Description: The host pools with live statistics on the interface, only the pools the user can access. rsp is an array of records: pool_id, pool_name, hosts (active hosts), seen_since (seconds since the first packet), bytes_sent, bytes_rcvd, traffic (bytes), throughput (bps), members (as stored: <address>/<prefix>@<vlan> or MAC), members_labels (members without the @<vlan> suffix). recordsTotal/recordsFiltered: number of pools.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/interface/lightview/data.lua

Get interface data aggregated from all interfaces (Overview)

  • Description: Interface data aggregated (summed) across all the interfaces, as shown by the Overview (lightview) mode in the Interface Details page. The response is a subset of /lua/rest/v2/get/interface/data.lua, plus the list of aggregated interfaces. Export stats (db, es, kafka, syslog) are reported only when the corresponding export is enabled, alert counters only when the user has the alerts capability, ZMQ counters only when at least one interface is a collector.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/interface/config.lua

Get the settings of an interface

  • Description: Administrators only. The settings that apply to the interface and their values: custom_name, speed (Mbit/s), scaling_factor, lbd_by_mac (hosts of the local broadcast domain identified by MAC; lbd_locked when forced to IP), top_talkers, flow_dump, automatic_reports, mirrored and gw_macs, network_discovery, companion and companions, recording_provider and recording_providers, disaggregation and disaggregation_modes, show_dyn_traffic, push_host_filters. A setting missing from the answer does not apply to the interface. Not for pcap files.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

Responses

0 - OK

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE (missing, not allowed or a pcap file)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED (administrators only)

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/interface/overview.lua

Get the interface overview

  • Description: Identity and configuration of an interface, as shown by its Overview page: name, type, MTU and speed, paused state, connected nProbe instances, flow export backends. Administrators also get the traffic recording windows, the disk usage and the ZMQ encryption public key. The live counters are returned by get/interface/data.lua. With view=lightview (all the interfaces together) only is_light_view, can_reset, show_alerts, timeseries_enabled, exports, export_enabled, dedup_enabled and nprobe_page are returned (the aggregated counters are in get/interface/lightview/data.lua).

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier (default: the interface of the session)

integer

view

query

lightview: all the interfaces together

string

Responses

0 - OK: { ifid, name, label, description, mac, type, inline, has_traffic_directions, mtu, speed_mbps, is_view, is_sub_interface, is_packet_interface, is_pcap_dump, is_syslog, is_netfilter, is_paused, can_pause, can_reset, can_live_capture, num_probes, nprobe_page, show_alerts, timeseries_enabled, has_role_traffic, has_flow_collection, db_stats, dedup_enabled, exports: [{ id, label }], export_enabled, export_direct, zmq_encryption_key, recording, storage, external_stats, maps_available, is_asn_mode }

-2 - INVALID_INTERFACE

integer

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/timeseries/ts.lua

Get timeseries data

  • Description: Get timeseries data

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier

integer

epoch_begin

query

Start time (epoch)

integer

epoch_end

query

End time (epoch)

integer

limit

query

Maximum number of timeseries points

number

initial_point

query

A boolean used to return the first point of the timeseries or not

boolean

ts_compare

query

Choices available: ‘30m’, ‘1h’, ‘1d’. A string reporting the timeframe of the timeseries to be compared to (e.g. ‘30m’, means to return other then the timeserie requested, the 30 minutes before)

string

ts_query

query

Containing the parameters used to find the timeserie in the following format, parameter:value;parameter:value;… (e.g. ‘ifid:1;host:192.168.1.1’)

string

tskey

query

Containing the timeseries key, whom the timeseries is referred to (e.g. if the timeserie is regarding the interface ‘7’ or the host ‘192.168.1.1’, then tskey is going to be ‘7’ or ‘192.168.1.1’)

string

ts_schema

query

Contains the timeserie schema (e.g. ‘iface:traffic’)

string

Responses

0 - OK

-6 - INTERNAL_ERROR

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/issue/list.lua

List issues

  • Description: List the issues of a view, with filters, sorting and paging. Each record has issue_id, status (open|resolved), severity {id, label}, alert {id, name}, entity {id, label, value, name, host, role, url (host and flow issues: the host page on the first interface where the issue has been seen)}, ifids, interfaces [{id, name}] (where the issue has been seen), first_seen, last_seen, resolved_at, duration, signal_count, score (highest score of the alerts), total_score (sum of the scores of the alerts), peers_count, peers_sample, reopen_count, resolve_policy (on_release|quiet_period|manual), auto_resolve_at (open quiet_period issues: when resolved unless new alerts arrive), resolution, ack_user, ack_tstamp, assignee, suppressed, suppressed_until, tags [{id, name, color}] (union of the tags of the alerts of the issue, host and flow alerts only), alerts_url. The total is in recordsTotal. Enterprise XXL+ only, requires alerts enabled and the alerts capability.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

view

query

problems (open issues, default), recent (resolved in the last 24 hours) or history (all the issues active in epoch_begin..epoch_end)

string

epoch_begin

query

Start time (epoch, view history only)

integer

epoch_end

query

End time (epoch, view history only)

integer

severity

query

Severity identifiers, comma separated (e.g. ‘5’ or ‘5;eq,6;eq’)

string

alert_id

query

Alert type identifiers, comma separated

string

entity_id

query

Alert entity identifiers, comma separated (e.g. 1 host, 4 flow)

string

assignee

query

Users the issues are assigned to, comma-separated (~: unassigned)

string

tag

query

Tag ids (0-63), comma-separated: the issues with one of the tags (tags of their alerts)

string

suppressed

query

Open issues: 1 the suppressed issues, 0 the not suppressed ones (default), 0,1 both

string

resolve_policy

query

Open issues: comma-separated ways they are resolved: 0 when the alert ends, 1 after 24 hours without alerts, 2 only by a user

string

opened_within

query

Only the issues first seen in the last seconds (e.g. 3600). Heatmap: also its time window (3600, 86400 or 172800, the default)

integer

map_search

query

Text searched in the entity (e.g. part of an IP), or an issue ID (e.g. 1573 or #1573)

string

host

query

Host (IP or IP@VLAN): only the issues of the host and the flow issues about it

string

alert_category

query

Alert categories, comma-separated

string

mitre_tactic

query

MITRE tactic ids of the alert types, comma-separated (0: no tactic)

string

problem

query

Alert types as <alert entity id>:<alert id>, comma-separated (e.g. 4:102,1:3)

string

issue_entity

query

Entity of the issues as <alert entity id>|<entity value> (e.g. 0|12 for interface 12)

string

issue_peer

query

Peer of the issues: an IP (the issues whose only peer is it), * (the issues with several peers) or ~ (the issues without peers)

string

show_suppressed

query

View problems: include the suppressed issues (default false)

boolean

only_suppressed

query

View problems: only the suppressed issues (default false)

boolean

start

query

First record (default 0)

integer

length

query

Number of records (default 10, max 500)

integer

sort

query

Sort column: issue_id, severity, score, first_seen, last_seen (default), resolved_at, signal_count, total_score, peers_count, alert_id, entity_val

string

order

query

asc or desc (default)

string

Responses

0 - OK

-2 - NOT_ENABLED (not Enterprise XXL+, alerts disabled or Issues preference off)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS (unknown view)

  • Description: For each facet (entity_id, severity, alert_category, mitre_tactic, problem, assignee, tag: all the tags, those without issues last with count 0, suppressed and resolve_policy: problems view only), the number of issues of each value (at most 12 except tag, the largest first; severities the most severe first) among the issues matching the filters of the other facets. GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/issue/groups.lua

Issue groups

  • Description: Issues grouped by one dimension (group_by), the most severe groups first, then the largest. Each group has key, label, entity (entity type label, problem and host groups only), count, max_severity, last_seen, signal_count, total_score (sum of the scores of the alerts) and params: the parameters to add to list.lua to get the issues of the group, or to this endpoint for a further level. has_more tells if there are more groups. Same filters of the issue list. Enterprise XXL+ only, requires alerts enabled and the alerts capability.

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/issue/heatmap.lua

Issue heatmap

  • Description: Issue activity in a time window (opened_within: 1 hour, 24 hours, or 48 hours by default), in slots of 1, 15 or 30 minutes: slot (seconds), begin and end (epochs) and cells, a list of { tstamp, severity, value, issues } where value is the number of issues opened, reopened or escalated to that severity in the slot (events) and issues the number of distinct issues, only for the severities from min_severity (the minimum severity of the alerts opening issues, Preferences > Alerts). Same filters of the issue list, except the view and its time range. Enterprise XXL+ only, requires alerts enabled and the alerts capability.

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/alert/groups.lua

Alert groups

  • Description: Alerts grouped by one dimension (Alerts Overview and issue preview), on the Alert Explorer stores (ClickHouse or SQLite) merged with the live alerts in memory (engaged host alerts, active flows with an alert). Any Alert Explorer filter (e.g. alert_id=79;eq, ip=192.168.1.1;eq, cli_ip, srv_ip) narrows the alerts; each group returns key, label, count, max_severity, max_score, first_seen, last_seen, families, the distinct hosts and the filters that select it in the Alert Explorer. Enterprise L+ only, with the Experimental Features preference or the issues enabled, and the alerts capability.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

ifid

query

Interface identifier (default: the current one)

integer

epoch_begin

query

Start of the time range (epoch seconds)

integer

epoch_end

query

End of the time range (epoch seconds)

integer

group_by

query

Dimension of the groups (default alert_id)

string

page

query

Alert family: only its alerts (default: flow and host alerts)

string

status

query

Alert status: historical (require attention, default), acknowledged or any

string

sort

query

Order of the groups (default priority)

string

order

query

Direction of the sort (default desc)

string

query

Maximum number of groups (default 50, max 500)

integer

episodes

query

0 to skip the episodes count (ClickHouse only, default 1)

integer

episode_gap

query

Maximum pause (seconds) between alerts of the same episode (default 900)

integer

totals

query

1 to also return the totals of the grouping

integer

strict

query

0 to match also the flows where the alert_id filter type is only a secondary alert, as the Alert Explorer (default 1: predominant alert only, so that child groups add up to their parent). Live flows match their predominant alert only

integer

Responses

0 - OK

-3 - NOT_GRANTED (not Enterprise L+, feature not enabled or no alerts capability)

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS (time range, page or group_by)

-6 - INTERNAL_ERROR

POST /lua/pro/rest/v2/edit/issue/group.lua

Apply an action to a group of issues

  • Description: Applies the same user action to all the issues matching the filters of get/issue/list.lua (e.g. the params of a group of get/issue/groups.lua), written once. The issues the action does not apply to (e.g. resolve on a resolved issue) are skipped and counted in skipped by error id (not_found, not_open, not_resolved, newer_issue_open). An optional comment is added to each changed issue; with action comment only the comment is added, to all the matching issues. The changes are written in batches of 500. A group larger than the maximum number of open issues (preference issues_max_open, 50000 by default, as resolve_all.lua) is refused (error too_many, rsp.max), never changed in part. POST (JSON or form data, CSRF checked). Enterprise XXL+ only, requires alerts enabled and the alerts capability.

  • Consumes: [‘application/json’]

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Parameters

Name

Position

Description

Type

action

formData

The action: acknowledge, assign, suppress, severity, resolve, reopen or comment

string

issue_assignee

formData

assign: comma-separated users, empty to unassign (assignee is the filter of the issues)

string

duration

formData

suppress: seconds, 0 to remove the suppression

integer

issue_severity

formData

severity: 3=notice, 4=warning, 5=error, 6=critical, 8=emergency (severity is the filter of the issues)

integer

comment

formData

Comment added to each changed issue (max 4096 characters), required with action comment

string

view

formData

problems (default), recent or history (with epoch_begin, epoch_end)

string

host

formData

Filter: IP or IP@VLAN. The other filters of get/issue/list.lua (problem, severity, entity_id, alert_category, mitre_tactic, assignee, tag, suppressed, resolve_policy, issue_peer, issue_entity, opened_within) are accepted too

string

Responses

0 - OK: { matched (issues of the group), done (issues changed), skipped (error id -> number of issues) }

-2 - NOT_ENABLED (not Enterprise XXL+, alerts disabled or Issues preference off)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS (rsp.error too_many: more than rsp.max issues)

-6 - INTERNAL_ERROR (the comment could not be written)

POST /lua/pro/rest/v2/edit/issue/severity.lua

Change the severity of an issue

  • Description: Set the severity of an open issue (3=notice, 4=warning, 5=error, 6=critical, 8=emergency), e.g. to escalate it. A higher severity is an escalation: the acknowledgement is cleared and the recipients of the issues are notified. Later alerts more severe than the chosen severity still raise it. Recorded in the issue events (severity) with the user. Enterprise XXL+ only, requires alerts enabled and the alerts capability.

  • Consumes: [‘application/json’]

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

-2 - NOT_ENABLED (not Enterprise XXL+, alerts disabled or Issues preference off)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-1 - NOT_FOUND

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS: rsp.error tells why (not_open, not_resolved, newer_issue_open, expired)

POST /lua/pro/rest/v2/edit/issue/resolve_all.lua

Resolve all the issues matching the filters

  • Description: Resolves all the open issues matching the filters of the issue list (view problems; same filters as get/issue/list.lua: entity_id, severity, alert_category, mitre_tactic, problem, assignee, tag, suppressed, resolve_policy, opened_within, map_search, host, issue_entity, issue_peer), as the Resolve All button of the issues page. Each one is resolved as by edit/issue/resolve.lua (resolution manual, recipients notified), with the optional comment added to its timeline. Answer: resolved (issues resolved) and matching (issues matching the filters; the others were resolved meantime). POST (JSON or form data, CSRF checked). Enterprise XXL+ only, requires alerts enabled and the alerts capability.

  • Consumes: [‘application/json’]

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

string

string

Responses

-2 - NOT_ENABLED (not Enterprise XXL+, alerts disabled or Issues preference off)

-3 - NOT_GRANTED

-5 - INVALID_ARGUMENTS (view other than problems, invalid filters)

-6 - INTERNAL_ERROR (the issues were resolved, the comment could not be written)

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/host/hosts_details_by_port.lua

Get the hosts serving a port

  • Description: The hosts serving a server port in the live flows (ip, name, vlan_id, mac, mac_manufacturer, flows, score, tot_traffic), paged (recordsTotal). A missing or stale application/port falls back to the first available one: the answer has the values used (protocol, application, port) and the options of the page filters, counted on all the hosts of the port: applications [{id, name}], ports [{port, n_hosts}] of the application, vlans [{id, name, count}], manufacturers [{name, count}]. Enterprise L.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

Position

Description

string

query

integer

query

Only the hosts of this VLAN

integer

manufacturer

string

string

asc or desc

string

start

query

integer

GET /lua/pro/rest/v2/get/vlan/live_ports.lua

Get the server ports by VLAN (live)

  • Description: Sankey of the live flows: nodes (node_id vlan_<id> / port_<port>, label, link) and links (VLAN -> server port, label = application, value = bytes, l4_proto, l7_proto, optional_info.link to the live flows), the links with the most traffic first; vlans [{id, name}]: all the VLANs with flows; max_entries_reached when cut at limit.

  • Produces: [‘application/json’]

GET /lua/rest/v2/get/flow/bgp/looking_glass.lua

Get the BGP routes of an IP address

  • Description: The routes of the BGP RIB entry of an IP address (BGP feed), one row per prefix and peer: prefix, asn (of the IP), peer, peer_name and peer_site (when known), is_best_path, peer_asn { asn, name }, origin, as_path [{ asn, name }], next_hop, med, local_pref and communities.
